Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.\n4\t\n5\t**Goal:** The app drives both the Razer Naga V2 Pro and the Naga V3 Pro through one device layer and shows the detected model name everywhere the UI currently hardcodes \"Naga V2 Pro\".\n6\t\n7\t**Architecture:** A static model table in `RazerProtocol` (wired PID, wireless PID, display name) feeds a flat PID scan order (all wired PIDs before all wireless; V3 before V2 within a link). `RazerDevice` records the winning model's name and carries it through `BatteryReading` → `BatteryMonitor` → `DeviceState` → the four UI consumers. Probe loops iterate the same table. All command constants stay shared — the 2026-08-19 hardware probe proved the two protocols byte-identical.\n8\t\n9\t**Tech Stack:** .NET 10 (`net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0`), C#, WPF + WinForms, HidSharp, xUnit.\n10\t\n11\t**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-19-naga-v3pro-dual-support-design.md`\n12\t\n13\t## Global Constraints\n14\t\n15\t- Hard rule 1 (GATING): no new timers/threads/polling; device I/O stays on-demand inside `EnsureConnectedAsync`; ~0% idle CPU, ~23 MB private WS, zero input-feel regression.\n16\t- Hard rule 2: `AppHost.RestoreRawAsync` and the sweep snapshot are untouched.\n17\t- Hard rule 3: no `DropShadowEffect`/`BitmapEffect`; no hardcoded colors in themed XAML.\n18\t- Hard rule 4: README + `docs/images/` screenshots update on THIS branch before merge.\n19\t- Hard rule 5: run-at-login stays the delayed scheduled task (`StartupRegistration.cs`) — no task touches it; keep it that way.\n20\t- Hard rule 6: TDD through `IRazerDevice`/`FakeRazerDevice`; `InternalsVisibleTo.cs` untouched; `RazerDevice` transport and probe commands are the deliberate no-unit-test exceptions.\n21\t- Offline / unknown-model UI fallback string is exactly **\"Razer Naga\"**; V3 display name is exactly **\"Naga V3 Pro\"**; V2 is **\"Naga V2 Pro\"** (spec §2).\n22\t- V3 PIDs: wired `0x00E7`, wireless `0x00E8` (spec §3). User decisions recorded in §2.\n6\t\n7\t## 1. Goal\n8\t\n9\tThe app drives **both** the Razer Naga V2 Pro and the Razer Naga V3 Pro through one device\n10\tlayer. It finds whichever mouse answers, reports which model it found, and every existing\n11\tfeature (battery, DPI, button remap, profiles, dashboard, probes) works unchanged on both.\n12\t\n13\t**Success criteria:**\n14\t\n15\t- Installed build on the V3 Pro: tray battery %, dashboard, DPI get/set, remap chips, profile\n16\t  card all work over the 2.4 GHz dongle AND over the cable, and the header flips\n17\t  Wireless/Wired on a cable plug/unplug without restart.\n18\t- Same build still passes the full test suite through `FakeRazerDevice` (V2 behavior is\n19\t  regression-locked by the existing suite).\n20\t- Dashboard, popup, tray tooltip, and toasts show the detected model name, never a hardcoded\n21\t  one.\n22\t- `--probe`, `--probe-dpi`, `--probe-buttons`, `--probe-profile` find and exercise whichever\n23\t  supported mouse is connected.\n24\t\n25\t## 2. User decisions (2026-08-19, settled — do not re-open)\n26\t\n27\t| Decision | Choice |\n28\t| --- | --- |\n29\t| Dual support vs clean swap | **Dual support** — scan both PID sets, drive whichever answers |\n30\t| Naming | App stays **\"Razer Naga Companion\"**; UI shows the **detected** model name; offline fallback \"Razer Naga\" |\n31\t| Stage art | **Keep the V2 render** (`naga-thumb.png`) for both models for now; a V3 render is a follow-up when the user supplies one |\n32\t\n33\t## 3. Hardware baseline (probe spike, 2026-08-19, real V3 Pro)\n34\t\n35\t- Identity: product string \"Razer Naga V3 Pro\", VID `0x1532`, **wired PID `0x00E7`**,\n36\t  **wireless (HyperSpeed dongle) PID `0x00E8`** — the same wired-is-one-below-wireless pattern\n37\t  as the V2 Pro (`0x00A7`/`0x00A8`).\n38\t- Control collection: the one collection whose max feature report length is **91 bytes**\n39\t  (report id + 90), same as V2. On the V3 it lives on interface **MI_03** (usage page\n40\t  `0x0001`, usage `0x0003`); on the V2 it was MI_00. **Never key on interface number or\n41\t  usage — the 91-byte feature length is the selector**, exactly what `FindControlPaths`\n42\t  already does.\n43\t- Transaction id `0x1f` (first in `TransactionIdProbeSet`) works.\n44\t- Full parity, verified with write round-trips (originals restored):\n45\t  - Battery `0x07/0x80` + charging `0x07/0x84` — battery read 60% on both links; charging\n46\t    flipped 0→1 when the cable went in.\n47\t  - DPI get `0x04/0x85` / set `0x04/0x05` — 800×800 written, read back, 1600×1600 restored.\n48\t  - Buttons `0x02/0x8c`/`0x02/0x0c` — grid ids `0x40..0x4b` all answer; keyboard binding\n49\t    written to the volatile direct profile, read back byte-exact, original restored. The\n50\t    factory default binding is category `0x02` with a single `0x00` data byte (differs from\n51\t    the V2 factory default, harmless — raw category/data round-trips are what the app relies\n52\t    on, per the v2.3 snapshot/undo model).\n53\t  - Profiles `0x05` — list `0x81` (capacity 5), new `0x02`, delete `0x03`, and **this\n54\t    project's own** active-slot get `0x84` / set `0x04` all work on V3 firmware\n55\t    (create slot 2 → switch → verify → switch back → delete, every step acked `0x02`).\n56\t  - Device mode get `0x00/0x84` → normal (`0x00`).\n57\t- Same 91-byte report frame, same status codes (`0x02` success, `0x01` busy), same CRC.\n58\t- When the cable is plugged in, the `0x00E8` (dongle) collections disappear from enumeration\n59\t  and `0x00E7` appears — the OS-level flip mirrors V2 behavior.\n60\t\n61\t## 4. Design\n62\t\n63\t### 4.1 Model table (`Hid/RazerProtocol.cs`)\n64\t\n65\tA small readonly record + table; command constants stay shared (proven identical):\n66\t\n67\t```csharp\n68\tpublic readonly record struct MouseModel(int WiredPid, int WirelessPid, string DisplayName);\n69\t\n70\tpublic static readonly MouseModel[] SupportedMice =\n71\t{\n72\t    new(0x00E7, 0x00E8, \"Naga V3 Pro\"),   // daily driver first\n73\t    new(MousePidWired, MousePidWireless, \"Naga V2 Pro\"),\n74\t};\n75\t```\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `MousePidWired`/`MousePidWireless`/`DockPid` constants stay (probe docs, dock\n78\ttool, and the V2 row reference them). `DockPid` is untouched — the Mouse Dock Pro remains a\n79\tV2-era closed topic.\n80\t\n81\t### 4.2 Device scan (`Hid/RazerDevice.cs`)\n82\t\n83\t`FindControlPaths` keeps its `(int Pid, string Path)` shape and iterates a flat\n84\t`RazerProtocol.ScanPids` order — **every model's wired PID first** (V3, then V2), **then every\n85\tmodel's wireless PID** (V3, then V2) — with model name and link type resolved from the PID via\n86\t`RazerProtocol.ModelNameForPid`/`IsWiredPid` at commit time (amended 2026-08-19 in the plan\n87\tdebate; functionally identical to the tuple shape this section first sketched). Rules preserved:\n88\t\n89\t- Wired always beats wireless — a cabled mouse must win over a still-plugged-in receiver\n90\t  that can no longer reach it (existing rule, unchanged).\n91\t- The 91-byte feature-length filter selects the control collection.\n92\t- `EnsureConnectedAsync` already confirms a candidate collection with a real battery query before\n93\t  committing (`RazerDevice.cs:74`); that same check is what rejects the user's still-plugged\n94\t  V2 receiver when the V2 is idle. No new logic.\n95\t\n96\t`RazerDevice` records the winning model's `DisplayName` alongside the existing `_wired` flag\n97\t(`_wired` becomes `RazerProtocol.IsWiredPid(pid)`).\n98\t\n99\t### 4.3 Model-name plumbing\n100\t\n101\t- `BatteryReading` gains `string? Model` (null when absent) next to `IsWired`.\n102\t- `DeviceState` gains `string? Model` next to `Wired`.\n103\t- `BatteryMonitor` copies it through (`DeviceState.Online(...)`).\n104\t- Consumers replace the four hardcoded \"Naga V2 Pro\" strings:\n105\t  - `Ui/Dashboard/DashboardWindow.xaml:21` header → bound to a new `DashboardViewModel`\n106\t    property.\n107\t  - `Ui/PopupWindow.xaml:14` header → same via `PopupViewModel`.\n108\t  - `Ui/TrayIconController.cs:69-70` tooltip.\n109\t  - `Ui/Notifications.cs:10` toast title.\n110\t- Fallback when offline or model unknown: **\"Razer Naga\"**.\n111\t- The UI screenshot probe's fake states carry a model name so probe renders and README\n112\t  screenshots stay deterministic (\"Naga V3 Pro\" as the shown default).\n113\t\n114\t### 4.4 Probes (`Diagnostics/`)\n115\t\n116\t`ProbeCommand` (3 PID loops) and `ProfileProbeCommand` (1 loop + the wired/wireless label at\n117\t`ProfileProbeCommand.cs:867`) iterate `RazerProtocol.ScanPids` instead of the two V2\n118\tconstants, and print the model display name + PID they found. The wired/wireless label\n119\tderives from the matched model's pids, not from the V2 constants. `--probe-dock` is\n120\tuntouched.\n121\t\n122\t### 4.5 Out of scope (this pass)\n123\t\n124\t- V3 stage art / per-model art switching (waits for a user-supplied render).\n125\t- Any protocol addition (DPI stages, polling rate) — next roadmap item, after this merges.\n126\t- Repo/app rename, README branding beyond \"supports V2 Pro + V3 Pro\".\n127\t- Dock relay (closed, V2-era; re-open only via `--probe-dock` evidence).\n128\t\n129\t## 5. What does NOT change (hard-rule audit)\n130\t\n131\t- No new timers, threads, or polling paths; the scan-order change runs inside the existing\n132\t  on-demand `EnsureConnectedAsync`. Idle CPU / memory / input-feel gates untouched (rule 1).\n133\t- Button snapshot + raw-undo path untouched (rule 2) — and the V3's odd factory default is\n134\t  exactly why it must stay: undo restores raw bytes the app can't model.\n135\t- No theming changes (rule 3).\n136\t- README + screenshots ride this branch (rule 4): supported-models line, regenerated\n137\t  dashboard/popup screenshots (header text changes to the probe default \"Naga V3 Pro\").\n138\t- Startup registration untouched (rule 5).\n139\t- TDD through `IRazerDevice`/`FakeRazerDevice` (rule 6); `InternalsVisibleTo` untouched.\n140\t\n141\t## 6. Throws unless slot is 1..5 and dataSize is\n233\t    /// DataSizeProfileEdit (0x01) or DataSizeProfileList (0x06).</summary>\n234\t    public static byte[] BuildSetActiveProfileBuffer(byte transactionId, byte slot, byte dataSize)\n235\t    {\n236\t        if (slot < 1 || slot > 5) throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(slot));\n237\t        if (dataSize != DataSizeProfileEdit && dataSize != DataSizeProfileList)\n238\t            throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(dataSize));\n239\t        Span<byte> args = stackalloc byte[dataSize]; // zeroed\n240\t        args[0] = slot;\n241\t        return BuildReport(transactionId, dataSize, CommandClassProfile, CommandIdSetActiveProfile, args);\n242\t    }\n243\t\n244\t    public static byte[] BuildNewProfileBuffer(byte transactionId, byte slot)\n245\t    {\n246\t        Span<byte> args = stackalloc byte[1];\n247\t        args[0] = slot;\n248\t        return BuildReport(transactionId, DataSizeProfileEdit, CommandClassProfile, CommandIdNewProfile, args);\n249\t    }\n250\t\n251\t    public static byte[] BuildDeleteProfileBuffer(byte transactionId, byte slot)\n252\t    {\n253\t        Span<byte> args = stackalloc byte[1];\n254\t        args[0] = slot;\n255\t        return BuildReport(transactionId, DataSizeProfileEdit, CommandClassProfile, CommandIdDeleteProfile, args);\n256\t    }\n257\t\n258\t    /// <summary>Read-only class-0x05 probe GET (--probe-profile, spec 2026-07-18 §5.3/§7). Throws\n259\t    /// unless commandId has the get bit (>= 0x80) — the profile probe must be UNABLE to compose a\n260\t    /// write. dataSize/args are caller-specified because candidates carry their own documented shapes.</summary>\n261\t    public static byte[] BuildProfileGetProbeBuffer(byte transactionId, byte commandId, byte dataSize, ReadOnlySpan<byte> args)\n262\t    {\n263\t        if (commandId < 0x80)\n264\t            throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(commandId), \"get-half ids only (>= 0x80): the profile probe is read-only by construction\");\n265\t        if (args.Length > 80) throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(args));\n266\t        return BuildReport(transactionId, dataSize, CommandClassProfile, commandId, args);\n267\t    }\n268\t\n269\t    /// <summary>Validates a 91-byte reply: status byte then XOR CRC over buffer[3..88] vs buffer[89].</summary>\n270\t    private static ReplyResult ValidateReply(byte[] buffer91)\n271\t    {\n272\t        byte status = buffer91[1];\n273\t        if (status == 0x01) return ReplyResult.Busy;\n274\t        if (status != 0x02) return ReplyResult.Failed;\n275\t        byte crc = 0;\n276\t        for (int i = 3; i <= 88; i++) crc ^= buffer91[i];\n277\t        if (crc != buffer91[89]) return ReplyResult.Failed;\n278\t        return ReplyResult.Success;\n279\t    }\n280\t\n281\t    /// <summary>Validates a feature reply. value = report byte[9] (buffer[10]) on success.</summary>\n282\t    public static ReplyResult ParseReply(byte[] buffer91, out byte value)\n283\t    {\n284\t        value = 0;\n285\t        var r = ValidateReply(buffer91);\n286\t        if (r != ReplyResult.Success) return r;\n287\t        value = buffer91[10];\n288\t        return ReplyResult.Success;\n289\t    }\n290\t\n291\t    /// <summary>Validates a DPI reply and decodes X=buffer[10..11], Y=buffer[12..13] (big-endian).\n292\t    /// A decoded value outside 100..30000 is treated as Failed (defends against wrong-layout replies).</summary>\n293\t    public static ReplyResult ParseDpiReply(byte[] buffer91, out int dpiX, out int dpiY)\n294\t    {\n295\t        dpiX = 0; dpiY = 0;\n296\t        var r = ValidateReply(buffer91);\n297\t        if (r != ReplyResult.Success) return r;\n298\t        int x = (buffer91[10] << 8) | buffer91[11];\n299\t        int y = (buffer91[12] << 8) | buffer91[13];\n300\t        if (x < DpiMin || x > DpiMax || y < DpiMin || y > DpiMax) return ReplyResult.Failed;\n301\t        dpiX = x; dpiY = y;\n302\t        return ReplyResult.Success;\n303\t    }\n304\t\n305\t    public static int ScaleBattery(byte raw) => (int)Math.Round(raw * 100.0 / 255.0);\n306\t}\n307\t"}]},"uuid":"a419f4b2-1b41-4f47-af63-e79dd244de38","timestamp":"2026-08-19T23:17:48.701Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3926fb7b-8d90-45f3-860e-c8ee9612769e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"C:\\Users\\Brandon\\naga-battery-tray","sessionId":"2f51810c-16b9-413c-9fb8-1e91486b8048","version":"2.1.234","gitBranch":"master"}

Reuses a live connection cheaply; on (re)connect it tries\n57\t    /// each candidate collection — wired first — and keeps the first that replies to a battery query. The\n58\t    /// verify step is essential: the wireless receiver stays enumerated when the mouse switches to wired, so\n59\t    /// picking by enumeration order alone would lock onto a collection that can no longer reach the mouse.</summary>\n60\t    private async Task<byte> EnsureConnectedAsync(CancellationToken ct)\n61\t    {\n62\t        if (_handle is { IsInvalid: false } && _tid != 0) return _tid;\n63\t        CloseHandle();\n64\t\n65\t        foreach (var (pid, path) in FindControlPaths())\n66\t        {\n67\t            var h = CreateFile(path, 0, FileShareReadWrite, IntPtr.Zero, OpenExisting, 0, IntPtr.Zero);\n68\t            if (h.IsInvalid) { h.Dispose(); continue; }\n69\t            _handle = h;\n70\t\n71\t            byte tid = await ResolveTransactionIdAsync(ct);\n72\t            // ResolveTransactionIdAsync short-circuits on a cached id without touching this handle, so confirm\n73\t            // this collection truly answers before committing to it; otherwise fall through to the next.\n74\t            if (tid != 0 && await QueryAsync(tid, RazerProtocol.CommandIdBattery, ct) is not null)\n75\t            {\n76\t                _tid = tid;\n77\t                _wired = pid == RazerProtocol.MousePidWired;\n78\t                return tid;\n79\t            }\n80\t            CloseHandle();\n81\t        }\n82\t        return 0;\n83\t    }\n84\t\n85\t    /// <summary>Mouse HID collections exposing the 90+1 byte feature report (with their PID), wired (0x00A7)\n86\t    /// before wireless (0x00A8): a cabled mouse must win over a still-plugged-in receiver that can no longer\n87\t    /// reach it. The PID is returned so the caller can report the active link (wired vs wireless).</summary>\n88\t    private static IEnumerable<(int Pid, string Path)> FindControlPaths()\n89\t    {\n90\t        foreach (int pid in new[] { RazerProtocol.MousePidWired, RazerProtocol.MousePidWireless })\n91\t            foreach (var dev in DeviceList.Local.GetHidDevices(RazerProtocol.VendorId, pid))\n92\t            {\n93\t                int max;\n94\t                try { max = dev.GetMaxFeatureReportLength(); } catch { continue; }\n95\t                if (max == RazerProtocol.BufferLength) yield return (pid, dev.DevicePath);\n96\t            }\n97\t    }\n98\t\n99\t    /// <summary>Returns cached id, else probes the set and caches the winner. 0 = could not resolve.</summary>\n100\t    private async Task<byte> ResolveTransactionIdAsync(CancellationToken ct)\n101\t    {\n102\t        var cached = _settings.GetCachedTransactionId();\n103\t        if (cached is not null) return cached.Value;\n104\t\n105\t        foreach (byte tid in RazerProtocol.TransactionIdProbeSet)\n106\t        {\n107\t            var value = await QueryAsync(tid, RazerProtocol.CommandIdBattery, ct);\n108\t            if (value is not null && RazerProtocol.ScaleBattery(value.Value) is >= 0 and <= 100)\n109\t            {\n110\t                _settings.SetCachedTransactionId(tid);\n111\t                return tid;\n112\t            }\n113\t        }\n114\t        return 0;\n115\t    }\n116\t\n117\t    /// <summary>One SET->wait->GET round-trip with one busy retry. Kept\n144\t    /// separate from ExchangeAsync so battery/DPI/writes and the transaction-id probe keep their\n145\t    /// proven timing semantics exactly.</summary>\n146\t    private async Task<byte[]?> FastReadAsync(byte[] request, CancellationToken ct, int echoArgBytes = 0)\n147\t    {\n148\t        if (_handle is null || _handle.IsInvalid) return null;\n149\t        if (!HidD_SetFeature(_handle, request, request.Length)) { CloseHandle(); return null; }\n150\t\n151\t        int delay = FastReadInitialDelayMs;\n152\t        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < 4; attempt++)\n153\t        {\n154\t            await Task.Delay(delay, ct);\n155\t            var reply = new byte[RazerProtocol.BufferLength];\n156\t            reply[0] = 0x00;\n157\t            if (!HidD_GetFeature(_handle, reply, reply.Length)) { CloseHandle(); return null; }\n158\t            // ready = a completed status AND the reply echoes OUR class+cmd (buffer[7]/[8] =\n159\t            // report class/cmd — every Razer reply echoes them; the parses already rely on it).\n160\t            // echoArgBytes extends the check over the request's leading arg bytes (buffer[9..]):\n161\t            // the grid sweep issues 12 same-class/cmd requests back-to-back, so a stale finished\n162\t            // reply from the PREVIOUS button would otherwise pass and read as a hard failure at\n163\t            // the parse layer instead of \"wait longer\" (review find). Only usable when the\n164\t            // request args are echoed rather than replaced by reply data (button reads echo\n165\t            // profile/buttonId/hypershift; list/active reads carry data there — pass 0).\n166\t            bool pending = reply[1] is 0x00 or 0x01\n167\t                || reply[7] != request[7] || reply[8] != request[8];\n168\t            for (int i = 0; !pending && i < echoArgBytes; i++)\n169\t                if (reply[9 + i] != request[9 + i]) pending = true;\n170\t            if (!pending) return reply;\n171\t            delay = Math.Min(delay * 2, 400);\n172\t
